# Language reviews and recordings still needed

Generated by `npx tsx scripts/export-language-reviews.ts --since 166a3ff`. Regenerate it after the next
batch of lessons lands rather than editing the tables by hand; the sections at the top are written by hand
in the script and say what is waiting on a person.

## What is waiting, and who does it

1. **Estonian, read by the native speaker** (ADR-005 amendment 6). Every Estonian line the lessons and their
   scenes added or changed since `166a3ff`: 2516 lines (C1 2516). Section 1 below. Mark
   each OK or write the fix; a fix goes into the lesson or scene file the lesson id names, and must still pass
   `npm test` (every word taught before it is shown). For B1 the lines to read first, what each part's author
   was unsure of and what a second model flagged, are in `docs/reviews/b1-flags.md`; for B2 they are in
   `docs/reviews/b2-flags.md`, and the native speaker reads every line of `b2-konekeel` and `b2-kultuur`;
   for C1 they are in `docs/reviews/c1-flags.md`, and every line of `c1-isiksus` and `c1-maa` is read.
2. **Recordings, by the native speaker.** The lessons are only partly recorded: the catalog in
   `lib/audio/native.ts` reaches lessons 1 to 13 of A1, and a man's part plays the native voice only where every
   line of it is recorded. Section 2 lists 5420 lines and words no recording says yet (A1 681, A2 1015, B1 1357, B2 1178, C1 1189),
   men's parts, model answers and taught words. The known retakes are listed first in that section.
3. **Russian and Ukrainian, read by fluent readers.** Both languages were machine translated, and every
   line was read and corrected on 2026-10-25. A line written or changed after that is not:
   `npx tsx scripts/export-translations.ts --unconfirmed --csv` lists those as a sheet, and
   `npm run apply:translation-review` reads a filled one back. Every line the app holds is in
   `docs/translations/ru.md` and `docs/translations/uk.md`.

Reviews already done are kept, with their verdicts, in `docs/reviews/`: the A1 and A2 Estonian and both
languages' A1 and A2 lines on 2026-10-25, and the same day every remaining written line in Estonian,
Russian and Ukrainian.

### C1: Being yourself

| 2 | Conversation, Tom | Anna siia. Ma ei julge vaadata... Ei, vaatan ikka. Ma sain... | Give it here. I don't dare look... No, I'll look. I got... | |
| 3 | Conversation, Anna | ...läbi? Ütle, et said läbi! | ...through? Tell me you passed! | |

| 5 | Conversation, Anna | Palju õnne, kallis! Ma ju ütlesin, et sa saad hakkama. Elagu Tom! | Congratulations, darling! I told you you'd manage. Three cheers for Tom! | |
| 6 | Conversation, Margit | Ja täna räägime sellest, kuidas õnne soovida. Tõnu, kuidas sina sõpra õnnitled? | And today we're talking about how to wish people well. {Tõnu}, how do you congratulate a friend? | |
| 7 | Conversation, Tõnu | Mina? Ütlen „palju õnne” ja kõik. | Me? I say 'congratulations', and that's all. | |
| 8 | Conversation, Margit | Nii lihtne? Minu vanaema kirjutas igale kaardile... | Just that? My grandmother wrote on every card... | |
| 9 | Conversation, Tõnu | ...„Kasvagu suureks ja olgu terve!” Minu oma ka! Varem kirjutasid kõik nii. | ...'May he grow up big and be healthy!' Mine too! Everybody used to write that. | |
| 10 | Conversation, Anna | Oi! Liis kirjutas grupis. Kuula: „Mihkel on siin! Sündis eile õhtul, 3,6 kilo. Ema ja poeg tunnevad end hästi.” | Oh! Liis has written in the group. Listen: 'Mihkel is here! Born last night, 3.6 kilos. Mother and son are doing well.' | |

| 36 | Words (word) | beebi | a baby | |
| 37 | How it works | Kasvagu suureks! | A wish for somebody who isn't the one you're talking to ends in -gu: {Kasvagu suureks!}, may he grow up big. It's the [käskiv kõneviis, ta] (command): one ending for him, her and them, and it is what a card, a cheer and a toast are made of. | |
| 38 | How it works | tehke, tehku, kasvama, kasvagu, olema, olgu | It's built like the polite command, with -gu or -ku where that has -ge or -ke: {tehke} gives {tehku}. For most verbs that is the -ma taken off and -gu put on: {kasvama}, {kasvagu}. And {olema} is short: {olgu}. | |
| 39 | How it works | Kasvagu poiss suureks!, Kasvagu lapsed suureks! | It's the same for one person and for many: {Kasvagu poiss suureks!}, {Kasvagu lapsed suureks!} | |
| 40 | How it works | Palju õnne!, Olgu ta alati terve!, perelisa puhul | Said to a friend it's short: {Palju õnne!} On a card it's the -gu form: {Olgu ta alati terve!} And in a card or a message from somebody older, {perelisa puhul}, for the new arrival. | |
| 41 | How it works | Las ta magab, Magagu ta! | {Las ta magab} lets him sleep, as at B2. {Magagu ta!} wishes it: may he sleep. A card and a toast say the second. | |
| 42 | How it works, table | Elagu Tom! | Three cheers for Tom! (long may he live), {ela} + -gu, from {elama} | |
| 43 | How it works, table | Kasvagu suureks! | May he grow up big!, {kasva} + -gu, from {kasvama} | |
| 44 | How it works, table | Magagu öösiti! | May he sleep at night!, {maga} + -gu, from {magama} | |
| 45 | How it works, table | Olgu ta terve! | May he be healthy!, {olema}, short: {olgu} | |
| 46 | Say it, question | Mida sa Liisile kirjutad? | What will you write to Liis? | |
| 47 | Say it, model answer | Palju õnne! Olgu Mihkel terve! | Congratulations! May Mihkel be healthy! | |
| 48 | Say it, question | Kuidas eksam läks? | How did the exam go? | |
| 49 | Say it, model answer | Ma sain läbi! Kõik läks suurepäraselt. | I passed! Everything went brilliantly. | |
| 50 | Say it, model answer | Palju õnne! Elagu Tom! | Congratulations! Three cheers for Tom! | |

#### C1 2. A hard week (`c1-02-sorry`)

| # | Where | Estonian | English | OK / fix |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 51 | Conversation, Helgi | Tere, Mari! Oi, Anna ja Tom on ka siin. | Hello, Mari! Oh, Anna and Tom are here too. | |
| 52 | Conversation, Anna | Tere, Helgi! Kuidas sul läheb? | Hello, Helgi! How are you? | |
| 53 | Conversation, Helgi | Ausalt öeldes mitte hästi. Mu vend Heino suri pühapäeval. | Honestly, not well. My brother Heino died on Sunday. | |
| 54 | Conversation, Anna | Oi, Helgi... Mul on nii kahju. | Oh, Helgi... I'm so sorry. | |
| 55 | Conversation, Tom | Meie siiras kaastunne. Kui me saame kuidagi aidata... | Our sincere condolences. If we can help in any way... | |
| 56 | Conversation, Helgi | ...siis ma ütlen, ma tean. Aitäh. Ta oli kaua haige. Me ootasime seda, aga valus on ikka. | ...then I'll say so, I know. Thank you. He was ill for a long time. We were expecting it, but it still hurts. | |
| 57 | Conversation, Mari | Helgi, mul on nii kahju. Mida ma saan teha? | Helgi, I'm so sorry. What can I do? | |
| 58 | Conversation, Helgi | Matused on laupäeval. Pärast tuleb meie juurde kakskümmend inimest. Kas sa saaksid teha kaks kooki? | The funeral is on Saturday. Afterwards twenty people are coming back to ours. Could you make two cakes? | |
| 59 | Conversation, Mari | Muidugi. Ära selle pärast muretse, ma teen kõik valmis. | Of course. Leave that with me, I'll get it all ready. | |
| 60 | Conversation, Helgi | Aitäh, kallis. Nüüd pean minema, veel on nii palju teha. | Thank you, dear. I have to go now, there's still so much to do. | |
| 61 | Conversation, Tom | Mida me nüüd tegema peame? Kas me läheme matustele? | What should we do now? Do we go to the funeral? | |
| 62 | Conversation, Mari | Kui Helgi kutsub, siis jah. Ja kirjutage talle kaart. Lihtsalt: „Siiras kaastunne. Mõtleme sinule.” | If Helgi invites you, then yes. And write her a card. Just: 'Sincere condolences. We're thinking of you.' | |
| 63 | Conversation, Anna | Ja lilled? Meil kodus viivad kõik matustele paarisarv lilli. | And flowers? Back home everybody takes an even number of flowers to a funeral. | |
| 64 | Conversation, Mari | Siin ka: kaks, neli või kuus. Paaritu arv on sünnipäevaks. | Here too: two, four or six. An odd number is for a birthday. | |
| 65 | Conversation, Tom | Hea, et sa ütlesid. Ma oleksin kolm lille toonud. | Good thing you said. I'd have brought three. | |
| 66 | Conversation, Mari | Ja kaardile võib kirjutada ka: „Puhaku ta rahus.” | And in the card you can also write: 'May he rest in peace.' | |

| 89 | How it works | Mul on nii kahju, Meie siiras kaastunne, Puhaku ta rahus | There's very little to say when somebody has died, and it's said briefly: {Mul on nii kahju}, I'm so sorry, then {Meie siiras kaastunne}, and then you stop. In a card the -gu form wishes the dead well: {Puhaku ta rahus}. | |
| 90 | How it works | Puhaku, puhkama, Puhka rahus | {Puhaku} is the -gu form of {puhkama}, to rest: a wish for the one who has died, said about them. On the ribbon of a wreath it's often said to them instead: {Puhka rahus}. | |
| 91 | How it works | Helgi ütles, et Tom tulgu kell kaksteist | The -gu form also passes on what somebody asked: {Helgi ütles, et Tom tulgu kell kaksteist}, Helgi said Tom should come at twelve. | |
| 92 | How it works | Mul on kahju, Ta oli ju vana | {Mul on kahju} is the right thing to say, and it's enough. Leave out {Ta oli ju vana} and every other reason it isn't so bad: that's for the family to say, if anybody says it. | |
| 93 | How it works, table | Mul on nii kahju. | I'm so sorry., face to face, first | |
| 94 | How it works, table | Meie siiras kaastunne. | Our sincere condolences., face to face, or in a card | |
| 95 | How it works, table | Mõtleme sinule. | We're thinking of you., in a card to a friend | |
| 96 | How it works, table | Puhaku ta rahus. | May he rest in peace., in a card | |
| 97 | How it works, table | Mälestame. | We remember him., in the paper, under the name | |
| 98 | Say it, question | Mu vend suri pühapäeval. | My brother died on Sunday. | |
| 99 | Say it, model answer | Mul on nii kahju. Meie siiras kaastunne. | I'm so sorry. Our sincere condolences. | |
| 100 | Say it, model answer | Siiras kaastunne. Mõtleme sinule. | Sincere condolences. We're thinking of you. | |
| 101 | Say it, model answer | Mitu lille matustele viiakse? | How many flowers do you take to a funeral? | |

#### C1 3. Is that a joke? (`c1-03-pun`)

| # | Where | Estonian | English | OK / fix |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 102 | Conversation, Mari | Palun, Tom, sinu tee. Aga ole ettevaatlik: tee on täna väga libe! | Here you go, Tom, your tea. But be careful: the road's very slippery today! | |
| 103 | Conversation, Tom | Libe? Ma ei saa aru. Tee on ju kuum, mitte... | Slippery? I don't get it. The tea's hot, isn't it, not... | |
| 104 | Conversation, Kati | ...libe! Tom, mõtle natuke! | ...slippery! Tom, think for a second! | |
| 105 | Conversation, Tom | Ah! Tee nagu tänav! Sa tegid nalja. | Ah! {Tee} as in the road! You were joking. | |
| 106 | Conversation, Kati | Lõpuks! Tom, sa oleksid pidanud oma nägu nägema. | At last! Tom, you should have seen your face. | |
| 107 | Conversation, Mari | Sa vaatasid mind, justkui ma oleksin hulluks läinud. | You looked at me as if I'd gone mad. | |
| 108 | Conversation, Anna | Kodus vaatab ta mind ka tihti nii, otsekui ta ei saaks midagi aru. | At home he often looks at me like that too, as if he didn't understand a thing. | |
| 109 | Conversation, Tom | See on loll nali, Mari. Aga mul on sulle ka üks. Mitu kuud on aastas? | That's a silly joke, Mari. But I've got one for you too. How many months are there in a year? | |
| 110 | Conversation, Mari | Kaksteist, muidugi. | Twelve, of course. | |
| 111 | Conversation, Tom | Ei, ainult üks. Taevas on ju ainult üks kuu! | No, only one. There's only one moon in the sky, after all! | |
| 112 | Conversation, Kati | Oi, pole paha! Tom, sa räägid juba nii, justkui oleksid eestlane. | Oh, not bad! Tom, you talk as if you were an Estonian already. | |
| 113 | Conversation, Tom | Ah, ei. Eile oli raadios üks nali ja ma ei saanud midagi aru. | Oh, no. Yesterday there was a joke on the radio and I didn't understand a thing. | |
| 114 | Conversation, Mari | Raadios on nalja kõige raskem mõista. Kõik naeravad juba enne, kui nali on läbi. | Jokes on the radio are the hardest to get. Everybody laughs before the joke's even over. | |
| 115 | Conversation, Anna | Mina saan naljast aru alles siis, kui keegi seletab. Ja siis pole see enam naljakas. | I only get a joke once somebody explains it. And then it isn't funny anymore. | |
| 116 | Conversation, Kati | Seletatud nali on nagu külm tee. | An explained joke is like cold tea. | |
| 117 | Conversation, Mari | Või nagu libe tee! | Or like a slippery road! | |
| 118 | Conversation, Tom | Aitab, aitab! Ma joon nüüd oma teed ja enam midagi ei ütle. | Enough, enough! I'll drink my tea now and not say another word. | |
| 119 | Words (word) | libe | slippery: what a road is when it freezes | |
| 120 | Words (word) | nägu | a face; {oma nägu nägema}, to see your own face | |
| 121 | Words (word) | justkui | as if: {justkui ma oleksin hull}, as if I were mad | |
| 122 | Words (word) | otsekui | as if: the same as {justkui}, a shade more written | |
| 123 | Words (form) | vaatas | he looked, she looked | |
| 124 | Words (form) | hulluks | mad, as what you become: {hulluks minema}, to go mad | |
| 125 | Words (word) | loll | silly, stupid: {loll nali}, a silly joke | |
| 126 | Words (word) | paha | bad; {Pole paha!}, not bad! | |
| 127 | Words (word) | taevas | the sky | |
| 128 | Words (form) | seletatud | explained | |
| 129 | Words (word) | sõnamäng | a pun, wordplay: a joke on a word with two meanings | |
| 130 | Words (form) | mõtte | of the thought, of the meaning | |
| 131 | How it works | Justkui, otsekui, Sa vaatasid mind, justkui ma oleksin hulluks läinud, nagu | {Justkui} and {otsekui} mean as if, and what comes after them isn't true, so it takes the [tingiv kõneviis] (-ks, would): {Sa vaatasid mind, justkui ma oleksin hulluks läinud}. Plain {nagu} only compares two things that are both true. | |
| 132 | How it works | Justkui, otsekui, Justkui, otsekui | {Justkui} and {otsekui} say the same thing. {Justkui} is what you hear, {otsekui} what you read. Both are followed by the [tingiv kõneviis] (-ks, would), because what comes after them didn't happen. | |
| 133 | How it works | oleks, justkui ma oleksin hulluks läinud | For something past it's {oleks} and the [nud-kesksõna] (-nud, done), as at B1: {justkui ma oleksin hulluks läinud}. | |
| 134 | How it works | nagu oleks, Ta vaatas mind, nagu oleksin hulluks läinud | Spoken, people often just say {nagu oleks}: {Ta vaatas mind, nagu oleksin hulluks läinud}. It means the same. | |
| 135 | How it works | tee, kuu | A pun turns on one word with two meanings: {tee} is tea and a road, {kuu} is the moon and a month. Find the meaning the sentence wants, then the other one, and the other one is the joke. | |
| 136 | How it works | justkui, otsekui, justkui ma olin hull, justkui ma oleksin hull | After {justkui} and {otsekui}, never the plain past: not {justkui ma olin hull}, but {justkui ma oleksin hull}. | |
| 137 | How it works, table | Sa räägid nagu eestlane. | You talk like an Estonian., a comparison: it's how you talk | |
| 138 | How it works, table | Sa räägid, justkui oleksid eestlane. | You talk as if you were an Estonian., as if: you aren't one | |
| 139 | How it works, table | Ta vaatas mind, otsekui ma oleksin hulluks läinud. | He looked at me as if I'd gone mad., the same, a shade more written | |
| 140 | Say it, question | Mitu kuud on aastas? | How many months are there in a year? | |
| 141 | Say it, model answer | Ainult üks. Taevas on ju ainult üks kuu! | Only one. There's only one moon in the sky, after all! | |
| 142 | Say it, model answer | Sa räägid, justkui oleksid eestlane. | You talk as if you were an Estonian. | |
| 143 | Say it, model answer | Ma saan naljast aru alles siis, kui keegi seletab. | I only get a joke once somebody explains it. | |

|---|---|---|---|---|
| 144 | Conversation, Jaan | Tulge sisse! Oi, kui palju asju te tõite! | Come in! Oh, look how much stuff you've brought! | |

| 146 | Conversation, Tom | Ma saan hakkama, see pole üldse... Kurat! Mu varvas! | I can manage, it isn't at all... Damn! My toe! | |
| 147 | Conversation, Liis | Jumal küll, Tom! Kas sa said haiget? | Oh my God, Tom! Did you hurt yourself? | |
| 148 | Conversation, Tom | Pole hullu. Hirmus valus, aga pole hullu. | It's nothing. Terribly painful, but it's nothing. | |
| 149 | Conversation, Anna | Tom! Mark kuuleb kõike. | Tom! Mark hears everything. | |
| 150 | Conversation, Jaan | No nüüd on tal uus sõna. Homme ütleb ta selle koolis. | Well, now he's got a new word. Tomorrow he'll say it at school. | |
| 151 | Conversation, Tom | Pagan... Ma ei mõelnud. | Blast... I wasn't thinking. | |
| 152 | Conversation, Liis | Vaata, „pagan” on palju parem. Seda võib ka lapse kuuldes öelda. | Look, {pagan} is much better. You can say that with a child listening, too. | |

| 154 | Conversation, Jaan | Ei ole. Kõik ütlevad seda. Aga mitte lapse ees ega ülemusele. | No. Everybody says it. But not in front of a child, or to your boss. | |

| 161 | Conversation, Liis | Mihkel ärkas üles! Kurat... Ma mõtlen: pagan! | Mihkel's woken up! Damn... I mean: blast! | |
| 162 | Words (word) | kurat | the devil; and the commonest exclamation there is: {Kurat!}, damn! | |
| 163 | Words (word) | pagan | a heathen; and the mild exclamation: {Pagan!}, blast!, which is fine in front of a child | |
| 164 | Words (phrase) | jumal küll | oh my God, for goodness' sake: surprise or worry, and mild | |
| 165 | Words (phrase) | kurat võtaks | damn it all: real annoyance, and stronger | |
| 166 | Words (word) | ropp | rude, vulgar: {ropp sõna}, a swear word | |
| 167 | Words (word) | hirmus | terrible, horrible; spoken, also very: {hirmus valus}, terribly painful | |
| 168 | Words (word) | jube | dreadful; spoken, also really, very: {jube raske}, really heavy | |
| 169 | Words (word) | varvas | a toe | |
| 170 | Words (form) | varba | of the toe; {varbale}, onto the toe | |
| 171 | Words (form) | kukkus | it fell | |
| 172 | Words (word) | vanker | a pram, a baby carriage | |
| 173 | Words (form) | vankri | of the pram; {vankris}, in the pram | |
| 174 | Words (verb) | kiruma | to swear, to curse: {Ära kiru!}, don't swear! | |
| 175 | Words (verb, I form) | kirun | I swear | |
| 176 | Words (word) | ettevaatust | careful!, watch out! | |
| 177 | Words (form) | kuuldes | with somebody listening: {lapse kuuldes}, in a child's hearing | |
| 178 | Words (form) | vihaselt | angrily, angry: {kõlab vihaselt}, sounds angry | |
| 179 | Words (form) | hullemaid | worse ones, some of them: {hullemaid sõnu} | |
| 180 | Words (word) | toost | a toast: a few words said before everybody drinks | |
| 181 | Words (form) | toosti | a toast, as an object: {toosti ütlema}, to give a toast | |
| 182 | Words (verb) | tõstma | to lift, to raise: {klaasi tõstma}, to raise a glass | |
| 183 | Words (verb, I form) | tõstan | I raise | |
| 184 | Words (form) | klaasid | the glasses: {Tõstame klaasid!}, let's raise our glasses | |
| 185 | Words (form) | mõelnud | thought: {Ma ei mõelnud}, I wasn't thinking | |
| 186 | How it works | Pagan, kurat, kurat võtaks | An exclamation is strong or mild, and that decides where you can say it. {Pagan} is fine almost anywhere, {kurat} is fine among friends and nowhere near a child or a boss, and {kurat võtaks} is real annoyance. | |
| 187 | How it works | Hirmus, jube, hirmus valus, jube raske | {Hirmus} and {jube} are terrible and dreadful, and spoken they also just mean very: {hirmus valus}, terribly painful; {jube raske}, really heavy. That is how most people turn the strength up without swearing. | |
| 188 | How it works | Kiruma, Ära kiru! | {Kiruma} is to swear: a parent says {Ära kiru!} | |
| 189 | How it works | ropp sõna | There are stronger words than these, and an Estonian calls each of them {ropp sõna}. You'll know them from the tone when you hear them. This course doesn't show them, nobody expects a foreigner to use them, and you lose nothing by not saying them. | |
| 190 | How it works | Kurat, pagan, oi | {Kurat} is said at a thing, not at a person. At a box on your foot it's ordinary; said to somebody, it's rude. At work, in front of a child or to anybody older, say {pagan}, or just {oi}. | |
| 191 | How it works, table | Oi! | mild: surprise, or a small pain, anywhere | |
| 192 | How it works, table | Pagan! | mild: blast!, almost anywhere, with a child listening too | |
| 193 | How it works, table | Jumal küll! | mild: oh my God!, for goodness' sake!, with friends; an older person's word | |
| 194 | How it works, table | Kurat! | strong enough to be noticed: damn!, among friends; never to a child, a boss or a stranger | |
| 195 | How it works, table | Kurat võtaks! | stronger: real annoyance, alone, or among close friends | |
| 196 | Say it, question | Kas sa said haiget? | Did you hurt yourself? | |
| 197 | Say it, model answer | Pole hullu. Hirmus valus, aga pole hullu. | It's nothing. Terribly painful, but it's nothing. | |
| 198 | Say it, model answer | Pagan! | Blast! | |
| 199 | Say it, model answer | Lapse ees ja ülemusele ära ütle „kurat”. | Don't say {kurat} in front of a child or to your boss. | |

|---|---|---|---|---|
| 200 | Conversation, Mari | Anna, sa oled täna nii vaikne. Kas kõik on korras? | Anna, you're so quiet today. Is everything all right? | |
| 201 | Conversation, Anna | Ma ei tea. Olen kuidagi... kurb. | I don't know. I'm sort of... sad. | |

| 203 | Conversation, Anna | Ei, kõik on korras. Kurb pole ka õige sõna. Pigem nukker. | No, everything's fine. Sad isn't the right word either. Low, more like. | |

| 205 | Conversation, Anna | Jah, aga mitte ainult ilm. Ma igatsen oma ema. Me pole kaks aastat kohtunud. | Yes, but not just the weather. I miss my mother. We haven't seen each other for two years. | |
| 206 | Conversation, Kati | Küllap igatseb ta sind ka. | I'm sure she misses you too. | |
| 207 | Conversation, Anna | Arvatavasti küll. Telefonis ütleb ta alati, et kõik on hästi. Aga ma kuulen, et ta on... | Probably, yes. On the phone she always says everything's fine. But I can hear she's... | |
| 208 | Conversation, Mari | ...kurvameelne? | ...sad, in a quiet way? | |
| 209 | Conversation, Anna | Jah, just. Kurvameelne. Ja mina olen pettunud, sest ma lootsin, et ta tuleb talvel siia. | Yes, exactly. Wistful. And I'm disappointed, because I'd hoped she'd come here this winter. | |
| 210 | Conversation, Tom | Tere! Anna, miks sul nii tõsine nägu on? | Hello! Anna, why such a serious face? | |
| 211 | Conversation, Kati | Anna räägib, kuidas tal tegelikult läheb. | Anna's telling us how she's really doing. | |
| 212 | Conversation, Tom | Nähtavasti mitte väga hästi. Sa oled terve nädal vaikne olnud. | Not very well, by the look of it. You've been quiet all week. | |
| 213 | Conversation, Anna | Aga ma olen ka tänulik. Mul on hea töö, tore pere ja head sõbrad. | But I'm grateful too. I've got a good job, a lovely family and good friends. | |
| 214 | Conversation, Mari | Vaata: kaks tunnet korraga. Nii see elu ju on. | See: two feelings at once. That's life, isn't it. | |
| 215 | Conversation, Tom | Kirjuta Liisile. Ta on kodus ega tea veel midagi Helgi vennast. Kirjuta talle mõlemad: see, mis on hea, ja see, mis on raske. | Write to Liis. She's at home and doesn't know about Helgi's brother yet. Write her both: what's good, and what's hard. | |
| 216 | Conversation, Anna | Kiri? Ma pole ammu kirja kirjutanud. Aga jah... küllap ta oleks väga üllatunud. | A letter? I haven't written a letter in ages. But yes... I'm sure she'd be very surprised. | |
| 217 | Words (word) | nukker | low, gloomy: sad in a gray, November way | |
| 218 | Words (word) | kurvameelne | wistful, melancholy: sad in a quiet, lasting way | |
| 219 | Words (word) | pettunud | disappointed: something you hoped for didn't happen | |
| 220 | Words (word) | tänulik | grateful, thankful | |
| 221 | Words (word) | üllatunud | surprised | |
| 222 | Words (verb, I form) | igatsen | I miss | |
| 223 | Words (form) | lootsin | I hoped, I'd hoped | |
| 224 | Words (word) | küllap | I'm sure, no doubt: your own warm guess | |
| 225 | Words (word) | arvatavasti | probably, presumably: what's likely | |
| 226 | Words (word) | nähtavasti | apparently, by the look of it: what you can see | |
| 227 | Words (form) | tunnet | feelings, after a number: {kaks tunnet} | |

| 229 | Words (form) | uudist | a piece of news, after a number: {kaks uudist} | |
| 230 | Words (word) | armastus | love | |

| 232 | How it works | küllap, arvatavasti, nähtavasti, kurb, nukker, kurvameelne, pettunud | How sure you are is a word of its own, and each says where the sureness comes from: {küllap} is your own warm guess, {arvatavasti} is what's likely, and {nähtavasti} is what you can see. And a feeling has shades: {kurb}, {nukker}, {kurvameelne} and {pettunud} are four different ways of being sad. | |
| 233 | How it works | Kurb, Nukker, Kurvameelne, Pettunud | {Kurb} is plain sad. {Nukker} is low and gray, the way November is. {Kurvameelne} is wistful, a quiet sadness that stays. {Pettunud} is disappointed: you hoped for something, and it didn't come. | |
| 234 | How it works | vist, ilmselt, Küllap, arvatavasti, nähtavasti | B2's {vist} and {ilmselt} are still the everyday words. {Küllap} is warmer, and {arvatavasti} and {nähtavasti} are a shade more written. | |
| 235 | How it works | Küllap, Küllap igatseb ta sind ka, Küllap ta igatseb | {Küllap} often puts the verb straight after it: {Küllap igatseb ta sind ka}. {Küllap ta igatseb} is said too. | |
| 236 | How it works | aga, Olen nukker, aga ka tänulik | Two feelings can sit in one sentence, with {aga}: {Olen nukker, aga ka tänulik}. | |
| 237 | How it works | Nähtavasti, küllap, arvatavasti | {Nähtavasti} needs something to go on: a face, a voice, a coat that's gone. If you're only guessing, say {küllap} or {arvatavasti}. | |
| 238 | How it works, table | Küllap igatseb ta sind ka. | I'm sure she misses you too., your own guess, said kindly | |
| 239 | How it works, table | Arvatavasti küll. | Probably, yes., what's likely | |
| 240 | How it works, table | Nähtavasti mitte väga hästi. | Not very well, by the look of it., what you can see | |
| 241 | Say it, question | Kuidas sul viimasel ajal läheb? | How have you been lately? | |
| 242 | Say it, model answer | Kurb pole õige sõna. Pigem nukker, nagu novembris ikka. | Sad isn't the right word. Low, more like, the way you are in November. | |
| 243 | Say it, question | Kas su ema igatseb sind? | Does your mother miss you? | |
| 244 | Say it, model answer | Küllap igatseb ta mind ka. | I'm sure she misses me too. | |
| 245 | Say it, model answer | Olen nukker, aga ka tänulik, sest mul on head sõbrad. | I'm low, but grateful too, because I've got good friends. | |

| 309 | Conversation, Margit | Tere hommikust! Kell on seitse ja see on hommikuprogramm. Mina olen Margit. | Good morning! It's seven o'clock and this is the morning show. I'm Margit. | |
| 310 | Conversation, Tõnu | Ja mina olen Tõnu. Sel nädalal räägime sellest, kuidas me uudiseid kuulame. Margit, kust sina hommikul uudised saad? | And I'm {Tõnu}. This week we're talking about how we listen to the news. Margit, where do you get your news in the morning? | |

| 312 | Conversation, Tõnu | ...silmad lahti teed. Arvasin kohe! Aga nüüd uudised. | ...you've even opened your eyes. I knew it! But now, the news. | |
| 313 | Conversation, Margit | Valitsus arutab täna eelnõud, mis keelaks koolis telefoni kasutada. Kehtiva seaduse järgi otsustab seda iga kool ise. | The government is discussing a bill today that would ban using phones at school. Under the law in force, each school decides that for itself. | |

| 319 | Conversation, Tom | ...kooli viib. Jah, ma tean. Mina. | ...to school today. Yes, I know. Me. | |
| 320 | Conversation, Margit | Ja nüüd ilm. Täna on kõva tuul ja õhtul hakkab sadama. | And now the weather. There's a strong wind today, and in the evening it'll start to rain. | |
| 321 | Conversation, Tom | Kõva tuul? Siis lähen autoga. | A strong wind? Then I'm taking the car. | |

#### C1 11. For a friend who missed it (`c1-11-summary`)

| # | Where | Estonian | English | OK / fix |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 496 | Conversation, Mari | Tere, Tom! Kuule, kas sa kuulasid eelmisel nädalal hommikuprogrammi? Kõik räägivad sellest uuringust. | Hi, Tom! Listen, did you hear the morning show last week? Everybody's talking about that study. | |

| 498 | Conversation, Anna | ...mis on tegelikult minu märkmed. Tom ainult kuulas. | ...which are actually my notes. Tom only listened. | |

| 500 | Conversation, Anna | Lühidalt: eestlased magavad keskmiselt veidi alla seitsme tunni ja noored kõige vähem. | In short: Estonians sleep a little under seven hours on average, and the young the least. | |
| 501 | Conversation, Mari | Ja telefon on süüdi, eks? Nii oli pealkirjas. | And the phone's to blame, right? That's what the headline said. | |
| 502 | Conversation, Anna | Ei! See on kõige tähtsam. Teadlane ütles, et uuring näitab seost, mitte... | No! That's the most important thing. The scientist said the study shows a link, not... | |
| 503 | Conversation, Tom | ...põhjust. Seda ma mäletan! | ...a cause. That I remember! | |

| 505 | Conversation, Anna | Muidugi. Kirjutan sulle kokkuvõtte. Mida sa veel teada tahad? | Of course. I'll write you a summary. What else do you want to know? | |
| 506 | Conversation, Mari | Kas ta midagi ka soovitas? | Did she recommend anything, too? | |
| 507 | Conversation, Anna | Soovitas küll: minna magama iga päev samal ajal ja jätta telefon õhtul teise tuppa. | She did: go to bed at the same time every day, and leave the phone in another room in the evening. | |
| 508 | Conversation, Tom | Seda ma küll ei luba. | That one I'm not promising. | |

| 510 | Conversation, Tom | Tapetakse? Keda? | Gets killed? Who? | |

| 512 | Conversation, Anna | Siis on Tom kindlasti süüdi. | Then it's definitely Tom. | |
| 513 | Conversation, Tom | Ma olen täiesti süütu! | I'm completely innocent! | |

| 641 | How it works | võrreldes, vastavalt, arvestades | Formal Estonian ties a figure to what it is measured against with a link made from a verb, and each link wants its word in a set form: {võrreldes} with -ga, {vastavalt} with -le, and {arvestades} with an object or a whole clause. | |

At work, and on the radio, the link comes in: {Võrreldes eelmise aastaga on hinnad kõrgemad}. In writing the figure goes with it: {Võrreldes eelmise aastaga on hinnad tõusnud viis protsenti}. | |
| 643 | How it works | Võrreldes, eelmise aastaga võrreldes | {Võrreldes} may stand after its word too, and both are right: {eelmise aastaga võrreldes}. | |
| 644 | How it works | Arvestades, Arvestades, kui palju me üüri maksame, oleks oma kodu odavam | {Arvestades} with a clause takes a comma: {Arvestades, kui palju me üüri maksame, oleks oma kodu odavam}. | |
| 645 | How it works | Vastavalt, prognoosi järgi | {Vastavalt} is the most official of the three. Said aloud, {prognoosi järgi} does the same job. | |
| 646 | How it works | võrreldes, vastavalt, Vastavalt prognoosile, vastavalt prognoosiga | Each link has its own form, and English gives no clue which: {võrreldes} takes -ga, but {vastavalt} takes -le. {Vastavalt prognoosile}, never {vastavalt prognoosiga}. | |

| 965 | How it works | nimisõna, tegusõna, omadussõna, alus, öeldis, nimetav, omastav, osastav | Mark learns Estonian grammar in Estonian, so he corrects you with the school's words: a word is a {nimisõna}, a {tegusõna} or an {omadussõna}, and in a sentence it does a job, the {alus} or the {öeldis}. The cases have their Estonian names too: {nimetav}, {omastav}, {osastav}. | |
| 966 | How it works | Nimisõna, tegusõna, omadussõna, alus, öeldis, Mark, nimisõna, Mark loeb, alus | {Nimisõna}, {tegusõna} and {omadussõna} are kinds of word; {alus} and {öeldis} are jobs in a sentence. {Mark} is a {nimisõna}, and in {Mark loeb} it is the {alus}. | |
| 967 | How it works | kes? mis?, alus, mida teeb?, öeldis, milline?, omadussõna | A teacher finds them with the question words: {kes? mis?} for the {alus}, {mida teeb?} for the {öeldis}, {milline?} for the {omadussõna}. | |
| 968 | How it works | See on ju omastav! | The cases are the names this app has used all along, [nimetav] (1st form), [omastav] (2nd form), [osastav] (3rd form), and at school they are ordinary words: {See on ju omastav!} | |
| 969 | How it works | Tegusõna, öeldis, tegusõna, öeldis, Mark loeb raamatut, loeb | {Tegusõna} and {öeldis} are not two words for one thing: {tegusõna} is the kind of word, and {öeldis} is its job in this sentence. In {Mark loeb raamatut}, {loeb} is both. | |

| 983 | Conversation, Piret | Tere õhtust! Siin Piret, esimese klassi õpetaja. | Good evening! It's Piret, the first-grade teacher. | |
| 984 | Conversation, Anna | Tere! Kas midagi juhtus? | Hello! Has something happened? | |
| 985 | Conversation, Piret | Ei, kõik on korras. Järgmisel neljapäeval läheme klassiga muuseumisse ja mul oleks vaja üht lapsevanemat appi. | No, everything's fine. Next Thursday we're taking the class to a museum, and I could do with one parent to help. | |
| 986 | Conversation, Tom | Neljapäeval? Mina olen terve päeva tööl, aga Anna... | Thursday? I'm at work all day, but Anna... | |
| 987 | Conversation, Anna | ...saab tulla, jah. Ma võtan vaba päeva. | ...can come, yes. I'll take a day off. | |
| 988 | Conversation, Piret | Väga tore! Kas te saaksite selle mulle ka kirjalikult kinnitada? Siis on mul kõik ühes kohas kirjas. | Lovely! Could you confirm it in writing as well? Then I've got everything in one place. | |
| 989 | Conversation, Anna | Muidugi, ma kirjutan teile täna õhtul. | Of course, I'll write to you this evening. | |
| 990 | Conversation, Anna | Kuula: „Tere! Saan neljapäeval ekskursioonile tulla kuid pean kell kaks ära minema et lasteaeda jõuda.” | Listen: 'Hello! I can come on the trip on Thursday but I have to leave at two to get to the kindergarten.' | |
| 991 | Conversation, Tom | Mark ütleks, et enne „kuid” tuleb koma. | Mark would say there's a comma before 'kuid'. | |
| 992 | Conversation, Anna | Ja enne „et” ka? Inglise keeles ma sinna küll koma ei paneks. | And before 'et' too? In English I wouldn't put a comma there at all. | |

| 994 | Conversation, Anna | Hästi, kaks koma juurde. Ja lõppu kirjutan: „Niisiis saan neljapäeval kella kaheni abiks olla.” | Fine, two more commas. And at the end I'll write: 'So I can help on Thursday until two.' | |

| 1002 | Words (word) | pealause | a main clause: one that stands on its own | |

| 1018 | How it works | et, kui, sest, mis, kes, kuigi, aga, kuid, niisiis, järelikult, ühtlasi, seejuures | Written Estonian puts a comma before every clause that hangs off another, whether you'd pause there or not: before {et}, {kui}, {sest}, {mis}, {kes}, {kuigi}, and before {aga} and {kuid}. And a long text ties one sentence to the next with signposts: {niisiis}, {järelikult}, {ühtlasi}, {seejuures}. | |
| 1019 | How it works | Ma tean, et ta tuleb., Tulen, kui saan., Õpetaja, kes helistas, oli Piret., kõrvallause, et, kui, kes | The comma comes from the grammar, not from your breath: {Ma tean, et ta tuleb.} {Tulen, kui saan.} {Õpetaja, kes helistas, oli Piret.} A {kõrvallause} after {et}, {kui} or {kes} is fenced off on both sides. | |
| 1020 | How it works | ja, ning, või, Tom ja Anna, aga, kuid, vaid | No comma before {ja}, {ning} or {või} when they join two things: {Tom ja Anna}. Before {aga}, {kuid} and {vaid} there always is one. | |
| 1021 | How it works | nii et, niisiis, järelikult | The same thing in three rooms: to a friend you say {nii et}, at work {niisiis}, and the most formal letter writes {järelikult}. | |
| 1022 | How it works | Ühtlasi, seejuures, ühtlasi, seejuures | {Ühtlasi} and {seejuures} both add: {ühtlasi} a second thing done at the same time, {seejuures} a detail that matters about the first. | |
| 1023 | How it works | Ma arvan et ta tuleb, Ma arvan, et ta tuleb | English leaves the comma out before 'that'; Estonian never does. On paper {Ma arvan et ta tuleb} is wrong, and {Ma arvan, et ta tuleb} is right. | |
| 1024 | How it works, table | niisiis | sums up: so | |
| 1025 | How it works, table | Niisiis saan neljapäeval tulla. | sums up: so | |
| 1026 | How it works, table | järelikult | draws the conclusion: so it follows | |
| 1027 | How it works, table | Buss läheb kell kaheksa, järelikult peavad lapsed varem kohal olema. | draws the conclusion: so it follows | |
| 1028 | How it works, table | ühtlasi | adds a second thing: also, at the same time | |
| 1029 | How it works, table | Tulen kaasa ja saan ühtlasi bussis aidata. | adds a second thing: also, at the same time | |
| 1030 | How it works, table | seejuures | adds a detail that matters: and what's more | |
| 1031 | How it works, table | Muuseum on lähedal, seejuures on pilet lapsele tasuta. | adds a detail that matters: and what's more | |
| 1032 | Say it, question | Kas te saate neljapäeval ekskursioonile tulla? | Can you come on the trip on Thursday? | |
| 1033 | Say it, model answer | Jah, saan tulla, aga pean kell kaks ära minema. | Yes, I can come, but I have to leave at two. | |
| 1034 | Say it, model answer | Koma käib enne iga kõrvallauset. | There's a comma before every subordinate clause. | |
| 1035 | Say it, model answer | Niisiis saan kella kaheni abiks olla. | So I can help until two. | |

| 1044 | Conversation, Giid | ...ja kevadel ärkab karu üles ja on väga näljane. Kes oskab öelda, mida ta sööb? | ...and in spring the bear wakes up and is very hungry. Who can tell me what it eats? | |
| 1045 | Conversation, Piret | Räägime sellest pärast, eks? Praegu panen ta Grete kõrvale. | Let's talk about it afterwards, shall we? For now I'll put him with Grete. | |
| 1046 | Conversation, Anna | Aitäh. Ma ei taha, et ta tunneks ennast... | Thank you. I don't want him to feel... | |
| 1047 | Conversation, Piret | ...üksi. Ma tean. Grete on väga hea paariline. | ...alone. I know. Grete is a very good partner. | |
| 1048 | Conversation, Giid | Nii, kes tahab karu lähedalt vaadata? | Right, who wants to see the bear up close? | |

| 1065 | How it works | kes, mis, Mark, kes istus üksi, oli rõõmus. | A {kes} or {mis} clause goes straight after the word it is about, and gets a comma on each side: {Mark, kes istus üksi, oli rõõmus.} | |

#### C1 23. A letter to the head (`c1-23-letter`)

| # | Where | Estonian | English | OK / fix |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1131 | Conversation, Piret | Tere! Rääkisin täna klassiga sõprusest ja sellest, et kedagi ei tohi välja jätta. | Hello! Today I talked to the class about friendship, and about how nobody may be left out. | |
| 1132 | Conversation, Anna | Aitäh. Kas läks paremaks? | Thank you. Has it got better? | |
| 1133 | Conversation, Piret | Natuke. Aga poiste klubi on ikka olemas. Ma soovitan teil direktorile kirjutada, siis saame kokku koos kooli psühholoogiga. | A little. But the boys' club is still there. I'd suggest you write to the head, then we can all meet with the school psychologist. | |
| 1134 | Conversation, Tom | Direktorile? Kas asi on nii tõsine? | To the head? Is it that serious? | |
| 1135 | Conversation, Anna | Piret ütles, et nii saab ka psühholoog aidata. See ei ole kaebus, vaid palve. | Piret said that way the psychologist can help too. It's not a complaint, it's a request. | |
| 1136 | Conversation, Tom | Hästi. Kirjuta siis: „Teie koolis kiusatakse meie poega ja keegi ei tee midagi!” | Fine. Then write: 'Our son is being bullied at your school and nobody's doing anything!' | |
| 1137 | Conversation, Anna | Tom! Nii ei saa. See ei ole ka aus, õpetaja on palju teinud. | Tom! You can't say that. It's not fair either, the teacher has done a lot. | |
| 1138 | Conversation, Tom | Olgu. Mida sa siis kirjutad? | All right. So what will you write? | |
| 1139 | Conversation, Anna | Kõigepealt, mis juhtus: „Meie poeg Mark jääb juba mitu nädalat vahetundides kõrvale.” Siis, mida me oleme ise... | First, what happened: 'Our son Mark has been left out at break for several weeks now.' Then, what we've done ourselves... | |
| 1140 | Conversation, Tom | ...teinud, et me rääkisime õpetajaga ja kutsusime ühe klassikaaslase külla. Ja siis? | ...done: that we talked to the teacher and had a classmate over. And then? | |
| 1141 | Conversation, Anna | Siis palve: „Sooviksime kohtuda Teie ja kooli psühholoogiga.” Ja lõppu „Lugupidamisega”. | Then the request: 'We would like to meet you and the school psychologist.' And at the end, 'Lugupidamisega'. | |
| 1142 | Conversation, Tom | Viisakas, aga kindel. Ja komad? | Polite, but firm. And the commas? | |
| 1143 | Conversation, Anna | Komad kontrollin ise. Seda kirja ei pea Mark lugema. | I'll check the commas myself. Mark doesn't need to read this letter. | |

| 1164 | How it works | Esiteks, Lisaks, Seetõttu, Ühtlasi, Niisiis | A formal letter to a school is polite and firm at once: it says what happened, what you have already done, and exactly what you ask for, and the signposts carry the reader from one to the next: {Esiteks}, {Lisaks}, {Seetõttu}, {Ühtlasi}, {Niisiis}. | |
| 1165 | How it works | palume, sooviksime kohtuda, palume kohtumist, võib-olla | Polite is the would form and {palume}: {sooviksime kohtuda}, {palume kohtumist}. Firm is a request with no {võib-olla} in it, and a date. | |
| 1166 | How it works | Klassijuhataja on meid palju aidanud. | Say what you saw, not whose fault it is, and say what the school has done well: {Klassijuhataja on meid palju aidanud.} A letter that is fair is a letter that is read. | |
| 1167 | How it works | Teie koolis kiusatakse meie poega!, Sooviksime kohtuda, et koos lahendus leida | Don't write in anger: {Teie koolis kiusatakse meie poega!} closes a door. {Sooviksime kohtuda, et koos lahendus leida} opens one, and asks for just as much. | |
| 1168 | How it works, table | Lugupeetud direktor! | Who it's to, Dear head, | |
| 1169 | How it works, table | Pöördun Teie poole seoses murega, mis on kestnud mitu nädalat. | Why you write, I am writing to you about a worry that has lasted several weeks. | |
| 1170 | How it works, table | Esiteks rääkisime õpetajaga. Lisaks kutsusime ühe klassikaaslase külla. | What you have done, First, we talked to the teacher. We also invited a classmate over. | |
| 1171 | How it works, table | Seetõttu sooviksime kohtuda Teie ja kooli psühholoogiga. | What you ask, firmly, We would therefore like to meet you and the school psychologist. | |
| 1172 | How it works, table | Ühtlasi palume, et psühholoog räägiks enne kohtumist meie pojaga. | A second request, We also ask that the psychologist talk to our son before the meeting. | |
| 1173 | How it works, table | Loodame Teie mõistmisele. Lugupidamisega | The close, We hope you'll understand. Kind regards | |
| 1174 | Say it, model answer | Lugupeetud direktor! | Dear head, | |
| 1175 | Say it, model answer | Pöördun Teie poole seoses murega, mis on kestnud mitu nädalat. | I am writing to you about a worry that has lasted several weeks. | |
| 1176 | Say it, model answer | Sooviksime kohtuda Teie ja kooli psühholoogiga. | We would like to meet you and the school psychologist. | |

#### C1 24. The meeting with the head (`c1-24-meeting`)

| # | Where | Estonian | English | OK / fix |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1177 | Conversation, Toomas | Tere tulemast! Istuge, palun. Piret rääkis mulle teie murest. | Welcome! Please, have a seat. Piret told me about your worry. | |
| 1178 | Conversation, Anna | Aitäh, et leidsite aega. Nagu me kirjutasime, jääb Mark juba mitu nädalat vahetundides kõrvale. | Thank you for finding the time. As we wrote, Mark has been left out at break for several weeks now. | |
| 1179 | Conversation, Toomas | Jah. Esimeses klassis juhtub seda igal aastal. Tavaliselt saavad lapsed ise hakkama. | Yes. It happens in first grade every year. Usually the children sort it out themselves. | |
| 1180 | Conversation, Anna | Ma saan aru, aga ma ei ole sellega päris nõus. See on kestnud juba mitu nädalat ja ise see üle ei lähe. | I understand, but I don't quite agree. It's been going on for several weeks now, and it isn't passing by itself. | |
| 1181 | Conversation, Toomas | Me ei saa ju lapsi sundida, et nad... | We can't force children to... | |
| 1182 | Conversation, Tom | ...sõbraks saaksid, jah. Aga seda me ei palu. Me palume, et kool aitaks neil koos midagi teha. | ...become friends, yes. But that's not what we're asking. We're asking the school to help them do things together. | |
| 1183 | Conversation, Piret | Mul on üks mõte. Ma võiksin vahetunnis teha mänge, kus kõik saavad kaasa teha. | I've got an idea. I could run games at break that everybody can join in. | |
| 1184 | Conversation, Toomas | Ja kooli psühholoog võiks temaga rääkida. | And the school psychologist could talk to him. | |
| 1185 | Conversation, Anna | Just seda me kirjas palusime. Ja et ta saaks ise öelda, mida ta tahab. | That's just what we asked for in the letter. And that he can say for himself what he wants. | |
| 1186 | Conversation, Toomas | Muidugi. Aga kas me peaksime siis kõik vanemad kokku kutsuma? Minu meelest on see liiga suur asi. | Of course. But should we call all the parents together, then? To my mind that's too big a thing. | |
| 1187 | Conversation, Tom | Ei, ma arvan, et seda ei ole vaja. Aitab, kui Piret räägib kahe poisiga. | No, I don't think there's any need. It's enough if Piret talks to the two boys. | |
| 1188 | Conversation, Toomas | Olgu. Niisiis: Piret teeb vahetunnis mänge, psühholoog räägib teie pojaga ja kahe nädala pärast saame uuesti kokku. | All right. So: Piret runs games at break, the psychologist talks to your son, and in two weeks we meet again. | |
| 1189 | Conversation, Anna | Väga hea. Aitäh teile! | Very good. Thank you! | |
| 1190 | Conversation, Anna | Tom! Mark ütles täna, et Oskar võttis ta vahetunnis mängu! | Tom! Mark said today that Oskar let him join the game at break! | |
| 1191 | Conversation, Tom | Tõesti? Ja kas ta on nüüd klubis ka? | Really? And is he in the club now too? | |
| 1192 | Conversation, Anna | Klubi enam ei ole. Nüüd mängivad kõik koos jalgpalli. | There's no club anymore. Now they all play football together. | |
| 1193 | How it works | Ma saan aru, aga ma ei ole sellega päris nõus., See on kestnud juba mitu nädalat. | Grant the point, then disagree: {Ma saan aru, aga ma ei ole sellega päris nõus.} Then give the fact behind it: {See on kestnud juba mitu nädalat.} | |
| 1194 | How it works | Seda me ei palu. Me palume, et... | When they answer something you didn't ask, say so and say it again: {Seda me ei palu. Me palume, et...} | |
| 1195 | How it works | Niisiis | Close by summing up what was agreed, with {Niisiis}, so everybody leaves with the same list. | |
| 1196 | Say it, question | Tavaliselt saavad lapsed ise hakkama. | Usually the children sort it out themselves. | |
| 1197 | Say it, model answer | Ma saan aru, aga ma ei ole sellega päris nõus. | I understand, but I don't quite agree. | |
| 1198 | Say it, model answer | Seda me ei palu. Me palume, et kool aitaks neil koos midagi teha. | That's not what we're asking. We're asking the school to help them do things together. | |
| 1199 | Say it, model answer | Niisiis: Piret teeb vahetunnis mänge ja kahe nädala pärast saame uuesti kokku. | So: Piret runs games at break, and in two weeks we meet again. | |
| 1200 | Role-play, they say | Tere tulemast! Piret rääkis mulle teie murest. Rääkige palun, mis juhtus. | Welcome! Piret told me about your worry. Please tell me what happened. | |
| 1201 | Role-play, model answer | Aitäh, et leidsite aega. Meie poeg jääb juba mitu nädalat vahetundides kõrvale. | Thank you for finding the time. Our son has been left out at break for several weeks now. | |
| 1202 | Role-play, they say | Esimeses klassis juhtub seda igal aastal. Tavaliselt saavad lapsed ise hakkama. | It happens in first grade every year. Usually the children sort it out themselves. | |
| 1203 | Role-play, model answer | Ma saan aru, aga ma ei ole sellega päris nõus. See on kestnud juba mitu nädalat. | I understand, but I don't quite agree. It's been going on for several weeks now. | |
| 1204 | Role-play, they say | Me ei saa ju lapsi sundida, et nad sõbraks saaksid. | We can't force children to become friends. | |
| 1205 | Role-play, model answer | Seda me ei palu. Me palume, et kool aitaks neil koos midagi teha. | That's not what we're asking. We're asking the school to help them do things together. | |
| 1206 | Role-play, they say | Mida te siis täpselt soovite? | So what exactly would you like? | |

### C1: Leading

| 1239 | Conversation, Tõnu | Nimelt sellest, milline on hea juht. Margit, kas sul on kunagi olnud hea ülemus? | Namely, what makes a good boss. Margit, have you ever had a good boss? | |

| 1245 | Conversation, Kadri | ...juhtima hakkab? Kas tuleb keegi väljast? | ...run us? Is somebody coming in from outside? | |

| 1270 | How it works | Meeskonda hakkab juhtima Tom, just, isegi, alles, nimelt | Estonian stresses a word by where it puts it, and by one small word in front of it, where English raises its voice. The news goes last: {Meeskonda hakkab juhtima Tom}. And {just}, {isegi}, {alles} and {nimelt} point straight at the word that matters. | |
| 1271 | How it works | Tom on nüüd meie juht!, Meeskonda hakkab juhtima Tom, Alates juunist juhib meeskonda Tom | The same news said to a friend, at work and in writing moves the same way: {Tom on nüüd meie juht!}, {Meeskonda hakkab juhtima Tom}, {Alates juunist juhib meeskonda Tom}. Whoever says it, the name comes last, because the name is the news. | |
| 1272 | How it works | Isegi, Isegi Anna ei tea, Alles, alles eile, Just, just Tom | {Isegi} is even and goes right before the word it's about: {Isegi Anna ei tea}. {Alles} with a time is only, as late as: {alles eile}. {Just} picks one word out of the sentence: {just Tom}. | |
| 1273 | How it works | Nimelt, Nimelt sellest, milline on hea juht | {Nimelt} says which thing exactly: on the radio, {Nimelt sellest, milline on hea juht} takes the topic and pins it down. In writing it opens a sentence that explains the one before. | |
| 1274 | How it works | vastutust võtma, usaldust kaotama, heaks eeskujuks olema | Each noun here comes with its verbs: {vastutust võtma}, to take responsibility; {usaldust kaotama}, to lose trust; {heaks eeskujuks olema}, to set a good example. | |
| 1275 | How it works | Tom hakkab meeskonda juhtima, just | Saying {Tom hakkab meeskonda juhtima} with your voice on Tom, the English way, stresses nothing for an Estonian ear. Move Tom to the end, or put {just} in front of him. | |
| 1276 | How it works, table | Tom hakkab meeskonda juhtima. | nothing in particular: a plain statement | |
| 1277 | How it works, table | Meeskonda hakkab juhtima Tom. | Tom: the news goes last | |
| 1278 | How it works, table | Just Tom hakkab meeskonda juhtima. | Tom and nobody else | |
| 1279 | How it works, table | Isegi Anna ei tea veel. | Anna, of all people: even | |
| 1280 | How it works, table | Ma kuulsin sellest alles eile. | how late it was: only yesterday | |
| 1281 | How it works, table | Nimelt sellest, milline on hea juht. | exactly what you mean: namely | |
| 1282 | Say it, question | Kes teie meeskonda juhib? | Who leads your team? | |
| 1283 | Say it, model answer | Meie meeskonda juhib Kristi. | Our team is led by Kristi. | |
| 1284 | Say it, model answer | Ma kuulsin sellest alles eile. | I only heard about it yesterday. | |
| 1285 | Say it, question | Milline on hea juht? | What makes a good boss? | |
| 1286 | Say it, model answer | Hea juht kuulab. Halb juht ainult räägib. | A good boss listens. A bad boss only talks. | |
| 1287 | Say it, model answer | Ma usaldan teid ja loodan, et teie usaldate mind. | I trust you, and I hope you trust me. | |

| 1318 | How it works | Rääkige palun natuke endast ja oma tööst | A job interview and the C1 speaking exam open the same way: {Rääkige palun natuke endast ja oma tööst}. | 1326 | How it works, table | Ma tahan ise kliente näha, mitte ainult koodi. | what you want next | |
| 1327 | Say it, question | Rääkige palun natuke endast ja oma tööst. | Tell us a little about yourself and your work, please. | |
| 1328 | Say it, model answer | Olen Tom ja olen pärit Inglismaalt. Elan Eestis juba mitu aastat ja töötan programmeerijana. Nüüd juhin ka meie meeskonda. Minu tugevus on see, et ma oskan inimesi kuulata. Minu nõrkus on aga see, et ma tahan kõike ise teha. Nüüd tahan õppida, kuidas meeskonda hästi juhtida. | I'm Tom and I'm from England. I've lived in Estonia for several years, and I work as a programmer. Now I'm also leading our team. My strength is that I can listen to people. My weakness, though, is that I want to do everything myself. Now I want to learn how to lead a team well. | |

| 1331 | Say it, question | Aga teie nõrkus? | And your weakness? | |
| 1332 | Say it, model answer | Ma tahan, et kõik oleks täiuslik. Aga ma õpin. | I want everything to be perfect. But I'm learning. | |

| 1335 | Conversation, Tom | Mul on täna raske vestlus ees. Marko kood hilines jälle. | I've got a hard conversation ahead of me today. Marko's code was late again. | |
| 1336 | Conversation, Siim | Teist korda sel kuul. Klient kirjutas mulle juba eile. | The second time this month. The client already wrote to me yesterday. | |

| 1338 | Conversation, Siim | ...kui sa teda ei solva. Ära ütle, et ta on laisk. | ...when you don't hurt his feelings. Don't say he's lazy. | |
| 1339 | Conversation, Tom | Ma ei arva, et ta on laisk. Ta on hea programmeerija. | I don't think he's lazy. He's a good programmer. | |
| 1340 | Conversation, Kadri | Siis alusta just sellest. Kõigepealt kiida, siis räägi probleemist. | Then start with exactly that. Praise him first, then talk about the problem. | |
| 1341 | Conversation, Siim | Ja räägi tööst, mitte inimesest. Mitte et sa jäid jälle maha, vaid et kood tuli kaks päeva hiljem. | And talk about the work, not the person. Not that you fell behind again, but that the code came two days late. | |

| 1343 | Conversation, Tom | Jah, istu. Kõigepealt: su kood on hea ja klient on sellega rahul. | Yes, sit down. First of all: your code is good, and the client's happy with it. | |

| 1347 | Conversation, Tom | Ma ei taha, et see korduks. Kas see sobib? | I don't want it to happen again. Is that all right with you? | |
| 1348 | Conversation, Marko | Sobib. Ma arvasin, et sa hakkad karjuma. | Fine by me. I thought you were going to start shouting. | |

| 1369 | How it works | kood tuli kaks päeva hiljem, sa oled laisk | Hard feedback is about the work, not the person, and it is precise: {kood tuli kaks päeva hiljem}, never {sa oled laisk}. Say what's good first and mean it, then the problem in one plain sentence, then what happens next. | |

| 1371 | How it works | Otsekohene | {Otsekohene} is direct, and Estonians value it: said kindly, one plain sentence is more polite here than a long English hint that leaves Marko wondering whether there was a problem at all. | |
| 1372 | How it works | kiitma, kriitikat vastu võtma, nõu andma, nõu küsima | The words come with their verbs: {kiitma}, to praise; {kriitikat vastu võtma}, to take criticism; {nõu andma} and {nõu küsima}, to give and to ask for advice. | |
| 1373 | How it works | Mitte et sa jäid jälle maha, vaid et kood tuli kaks päeva hiljem, mitte ... vaid | Siim's {Mitte et sa jäid jälle maha, vaid et kood tuli kaks päeva hiljem} is emphasis again: {mitte ... vaid} puts the two side by side, and the one that counts comes last. | |
| 1374 | How it works | Võib-olla oleks ehk hea, kui võib-olla... | Don't soften the problem until it disappears. {Võib-olla oleks ehk hea, kui võib-olla...} is kind to nobody: be gentle with the person and clear about the work. | |
| 1375 | How it works, table | Su kood on hea ja klient on sellega rahul. | starts with what's good, and means it | |
| 1376 | How it works, table | Aga kaks korda tuli kood hiljem, kui me kliendile ütlesime. | names the problem precisely | |
| 1377 | How it works, table | See on probleem. | says it plainly, once | |
| 1378 | How it works, table | Vaatame koos, mida ma saan sinu käest ära võtta. | says what happens next, together | |
| 1379 | How it works, table | Ma ei taha, et see korduks. | says what you expect from now on | |
| 1380 | Say it, model answer | Kõigepealt: su kood on hea ja klient on sellega rahul. | First of all: your code is good, and the client's happy with it. | |
| 1381 | Say it, model answer | Aga kood tuli kaks päeva hiljem. See on probleem. | But the code came two days late. That's a problem. | |
| 1382 | Say it, model answer | Mis juhtus? Vaatame koos. | What happened? Let's look at it together. | |
| 1383 | Say it, question | Kas sa oled kunagi saanud head tagasisidet? | Have you ever had good feedback? | |

| 1591 | How it works | iseenesest, teisalt, õigupoolest, kokkuvõttes | A whole case has four parts: what you claim, two reasons you develop, the other side's best case put fairly, and your answer to it. Four short words hold it together: {iseenesest} grants a point, {teisalt} turns to the other side, {õigupoolest} sets a fact straight, and {kokkuvõttes} weighs it all up. | |

#### C1 34. A day of volunteering (`c1-34-talgud`)

| # | Where | Estonian | English | OK / fix |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1708 | Conversation, Anna | Liis, Jaan, tere! Tore, et tulite! Ja Mihkel ka! | Liis, Jaan, hi! Great you came! And Mihkel too! | |
| 1709 | Conversation, Liis | Muidugi tulime. Talgud on talgud. Kus kotid on? | Of course we came. A volunteer day's a volunteer day. Where are the bags? | |
| 1710 | Conversation, Tom | Seal laua peal. Täna korjame prügi ja istutame puid. | Over there on the table. Today we're picking up litter and planting trees. | |
| 1711 | Conversation, Jaan | Puid? Kas see pole mitte linna töö? Iseenesest on talgud tore asi, aga... | Trees? Isn't that the city's job? In itself a volunteer day's a fine thing, but... | |
| 1712 | Conversation, Liis | ...aga linn peaks selle eest maksma, ma tean. Jaan räägib seda igal aastal. | ...but the city ought to pay for it, I know. Jaan says that every year. | |
| 1713 | Conversation, Jaan | Sest see on õiglane! Me maksame ju makse. Teisalt... | Because it's fair! We do pay our taxes. On the other hand... | |
| 1714 | Conversation, Anna | Teisalt? | On the other hand? | |
| 1715 | Conversation, Jaan | Teisalt on see teie park. Kui te selle ise korda teete, hoiate seda ka paremini. | On the other hand, it's your park. If you put it right yourselves, you'll look after it better too. | |
| 1716 | Conversation, Tom | Nii et kokkuvõttes oled sa talgute poolt? | So all in all you're for the volunteer day? | |
| 1717 | Conversation, Jaan | Kokkuvõttes olen ma siin. Andke mulle kott! | All in all, I'm here. Give me a bag! | |
| 1718 | Conversation, Liis | Vaata, lapsed tahavad ka aidata. | Look, the children want to help too. | |
| 1719 | Conversation, Anna | Las aitavad! Mark, võta see kott. Ja pärast on supp ja kook. | Let them! Mark, take this bag. And afterwards there's soup and cake. | |
| 1720 | Conversation, Tom | Ilma supita pole talgud talgud. | A volunteer day without soup isn't a volunteer day. | |

| 1746 | How it works | õiglane, kohus, vastutustunne | A question of right and wrong is argued like any other, with its own words: what is fair, {õiglane}; whose duty it is, {kohus}; and the sense of responsibility that makes people do it anyway, {vastutustunne}. Say what is fair, then what you owe, and weigh the two. | |
| 1747 | How it works | Õiglane oleks, kui..., Õiglane oleks, kui linn maksaks | {Õiglane oleks, kui...} with the would form is how you say what ought to happen without accusing anybody: {Õiglane oleks, kui linn maksaks}. | |
| 1748 | How it works | Kohus, linna kohus | {Kohus} is a duty here, {linna kohus}, the city's duty. The same word is also a court of law, and only the sentence tells you which. | |

| 1750 | How it works | Kokkuvõttes olen ma siin | {Kokkuvõttes olen ma siin} is a joke: asked for his verdict, he answers by picking up a bag. | |
| 1751 | How it works | Vabatahtlik, vabatahtlik töö | {Vabatahtlik} is a volunteer and also voluntary: {vabatahtlik töö} is unpaid work you choose to do, not work somebody gives you for free. | |

| 1765 | Conversation, Tõnu | Ja nüüd üks palve. Linnaosa leht kutsub kõiki kirjutama arvamuslugu peatänava kohta. | And now a request. The district paper is inviting everyone to write an opinion piece about the main street. | |
| 1766 | Conversation, Margit | Lugu võib olla kakssada kakskümmend kuni kakssada kuuskümmend sõna pikk ja peab rääkima kahest asjast: laste ohutusest ja parkimisest. | The piece can be two hundred and twenty to two hundred and sixty words long, and it has to deal with two things: the children's safety and parking. | |
| 1767 | Conversation, Tõnu | Parimad lood ilmuvad lehes enne avalikku arutelu. | The best pieces will be in the paper before the public hearing. | |
| 1768 | Conversation, Anna | Tom, see on meie võimalus! Me kirjutame selle. | Tom, this is our chance! We'll write it. | |
| 1769 | Conversation, Tom | Kakssada kuuskümmend sõna? See on ju terve... | Two hundred and sixty words? That's a whole... | |
| 1770 | Conversation, Anna | ...lehekülg, jah. Aga meil on kõik olemas: mõlemad pooled ja argumendid. | ...page, yes. But we've got it all: both sides, and the arguments. | |
| 1771 | Conversation, Tom | Ma alustaksin sellest, mida linn tahab. | I'd start with what the city wants. | |
| 1772 | Conversation, Anna | Ei, kõigepealt meie seisukoht. Lugeja peab kohe aru saama, mida me arvame. | No, our position first. The reader has to see straight away where we stand. | |
| 1773 | Conversation, Tom | Hea küll. Siis laste ohutus, siis parkimine ja siis vastuväide. | All right. Then the children's safety, then parking, and then the case against. | |
| 1774 | Conversation, Anna | Ja vastuväitele vastame ausalt. Iseenesest on poodide mure ju õige. | And we answer the case against honestly. In itself, the shops' worry is fair enough. | |
| 1775 | Conversation, Tom | Ja lõpus tuleb kokkuvõte... | And at the end there's the summing up... | |
| 1776 | Conversation, Anna | ...aga mitte üle kahesaja kuuekümne sõna! Lehes on selline reegel. | ...but not over two hundred and sixty words! That's the paper's rule. | |
| 1777 | Conversation, Tom | Siis on kõige raskem osa see, mida välja jätta. | Then the hardest part is what to leave out. | |

| 1796 | How it works | Minu seisukoht on, et, Esiteks, Teiseks, Vastuväide on see, et, Iseenesest, Kokkuvõttes | The paper's opinion text is a whole case in 220 to 260 words: your position first, then the two points you were given, each with a reason and an example, the other side's best case and your answer to it, and a summing up that weighs it all. Each part opens with words the reader knows: {Minu seisukoht on, et}, {Esiteks}, {Teiseks}, {Vastuväide on see, et}, {Iseenesest}, {Kokkuvõttes}. | |
| 1797 | How it works | Iseenesest | The case against is the other side's best one, put so that they would agree with how you put it. Then grant what is true in it with {Iseenesest}, and answer it. | |

| 1802 | How it works, table | Iseenesest on see õige mure. Õigupoolest on aga mujal läinud vastupidi. | your answer to it, In itself that's a fair worry. In fact, though, elsewhere it has gone the other way. | |

### C1: Estonia beyond Tallinn

#### C1 37. Down south (`c1-37-south`)

| # | Where | Estonian | English | OK / fix |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1865 | Conversation, Tõnu | ...ja kui te alles mõtlete, kuhu suvel sõita, siis Margit teab vastust. | ...and if you're still wondering where to go this summer, Margit knows the answer. | |
| 1866 | Conversation, Margit | Lõuna-Eestisse! Võrumaal ei ole lihtsalt teistsugune loodus, seal on ka oma keel. | To southern Estonia! {Võrumaa} doesn't just have different countryside, it has a language of its own. | |
| 1867 | Conversation, Tõnu | Ma käisin seal eelmisel suvel ja esimesel päeval ei saanud ma poolest jutust aru. Üks vanem naine küsis minult midagi ja... | I went there last summer, and on the first day I didn't understand half of what people said. An older woman asked me something, and... | |
| 1868 | Conversation, Margit | ...ja sina ütlesid „jah”, ma tean seda lugu! Aga kohalikud räägivad sinuga hea meelega ka tavalist eesti keelt. | ...and you said 'yes', I know that story! But the locals are glad to speak ordinary Estonian with you too. | |
| 1869 | Conversation, Anna | Tom, kuule! See on ju täpselt see koht, kuhu Kati meid kutsus. | Tom, listen! That's exactly the place Kati invited us to. | |
| 1870 | Conversation, Tom | Tema vanavanemate tallu? Ma arvasin, et see on kuskil Tartu lähedal... | To her grandparents' farm? I thought it was somewhere near Tartu... | |
| 1871 | Conversation, Kati | ...ei, ikka Võrumaal, metsa sees, järve ääres. Mul on ka raadio lahti! | ...no, it's in {Võrumaa}, in the middle of the woods, by a lake. I've got the radio on too! | |
| 1872 | Conversation, Kati | Mu vanaema rääkis seal kogu elu võru keelt. Ma tahan, et te kuuleksite, kuidas see kõlab. | My grandmother spoke the language of the south-east there all her life. I want you to hear what it sounds like. | |

| 1913 | Conversation, Giid | Nii, nüüd oleme raba ääres. Edasi läheme ainult mööda laudteed. | Right, now we're at the edge of the bog. From here on we go only along the boardwalk. | |
| 1914 | Conversation, Anna | Miks ainult mööda laudteed? Kas raba on ohtlik? | Why only along the boardwalk? Is the bog dangerous? | |
| 1915 | Conversation, Giid | Sammal on pehme ja selle all on vesi. Ja laugas on väga ohtlik koht. | The moss is soft and there's water under it. And a bog pool is a very dangerous place. | |
| 1916 | Conversation, Tom | Laugas? Ehk siis väike järv? Ja see seal, kas see on ka... | A bog pool? You mean a little lake? And that one there, is that one... | |
| 1917 | Conversation, Giid | ...laugas, jah. Selle kohta on isegi muistend. | ...a bog pool, yes. There's even a legend about it. | |
| 1918 | Conversation, Kati | Oi, räägi! Ma kuulsin seda lapsena vanaemalt, aga ei mäleta enam. | Oh, tell us! I heard it from my grandmother as a child, but I don't remember it any more. | |
| 1919 | Conversation, Giid | Räägitakse, et kunagi ammu olevat siin olnud küla. Inimesed olid aga muutunud ahneks ja... | They say that long, long ago there was a village here. But the people had grown greedy, and... | |
| 1920 | Conversation, Tom | Ahneks, ehk siis nad tahtsid üha rohkem? | Greedy, you mean they wanted more and more? | |
| 1921 | Conversation, Giid | Just. Ja ühel ööl olevat küla koos kirikuga maa alla vajunud. Hommikul oli seal ainult raba. | Exactly. And one night the village sank under the ground, church and all, they say. In the morning there was only bog. | |
| 1922 | Conversation, Anna | Ja see laugas? | And that bog pool? | |
| 1923 | Conversation, Giid | See olevat koht, kus seisis kirik. Vanad inimesed ütlesid, et vaiksel õhtul kuuleb vee all kella. | That's where the church stood, they say. Old people used to say that on a quiet evening you can hear the bell under the water. | |
| 1924 | Conversation, Kati | Nüüd ma mäletan! Vanaema ütles alati, et ahnus viib inimese rappa. | Now I remember! Grandmother always said that greed leads a person into the bog. | |
| 1925 | Conversation, Tom | Hea lugu. Aga kas see on päriselt nii olnud? | Good story. But was it really like that? | |
| 1926 | Conversation, Giid | Kes teab. Aga raba on siin igatahes palju vanem kui küla. | Who knows. But either way, the bog here is much older than any village. | |

| 1949 | How it works | Räägitakse, et kunagi ammu olevat siin olnud küla., Olevat, olid muutunud, Hommikul oli seal ainult raba. | A legend is told, not claimed: {Räägitakse, et kunagi ammu olevat siin olnud küla.} {Olevat} and the [nud-kesksõna] (-nud, done) say this is what people tell; the pluperfect, {olid muutunud}, says what had happened before; and the plain past is the storyteller stepping out: {Hommikul oli seal ainult raba.} | |
| 1950 | How it works | Räägitakse, et, Kunagi ammu | {Räägitakse, et} or {Kunagi ammu} opens it, and everybody knows a story is coming. | |
| 1951 | How it works | Olevat, olevat vajunud | {Olevat} with the [nud-kesksõna] (-nud, done) passes on what is told: {olevat vajunud}, sank, they say. You met it in B2 for news; in a legend it is the voice the story is told in. | |
| 1952 | How it works | Inimesed olid muutunud ahneks | What had already happened goes in the pluperfect: {Inimesed olid muutunud ahneks}, before the night it all sank. | |
| 1953 | How it works | Hommikul oli seal ainult raba, Raba on palju vanem kui küla | When the teller steps out of the story to say what is there now, it's the plain past or the present: {Hommikul oli seal ainult raba}, {Raba on palju vanem kui küla}. | |
| 1954 | How it works | Muistendi järgi oli raba kohal kunagi küla | A book says it with nouns and no voice at all: {Muistendi järgi oli raba kohal kunagi küla}. | |
| 1955 | How it works, table | Seal olevat kunagi küla olnud, mõtle! | To a friend, There was a village there once, apparently, imagine! | |
| 1956 | How it works, table | Räägitakse, et kunagi ammu olevat siin olnud küla. | A guide, to a group, They say that long, long ago there was a village here. | |
| 1957 | How it works, table | Muistendi järgi oli raba kohal kunagi küla. | A book, According to the legend, a village once stood where the bog is. | |

### C1: Ideas, books and the exam

#### C1 43. How does it end? (`c1-43-book`)

| # | Where | Estonian | English | OK / fix |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 2166 | Conversation, Tõnu | Tere hommikust! Täna on meie teema raamatud. Margit, mida sina praegu loed? | Good morning! Today our subject is books. Margit, what are you reading at the moment? | |
| 2167 | Conversation, Margit | Üht uut eesti romaani. Ja ausalt öeldes ma ei tea, kas see lõppes hästi või halvasti. | A new Estonian novel. And honestly, I don't know whether it ended well or badly. | |
| 2168 | Conversation, Tõnu | Oi, lahtine lõpp! Kas teile selline lõpp meeldib? Kirjutage meile! | Oh, an open ending! Do you like an ending like that? Write and tell us! | |
| 2169 | Conversation, Kati | Noh, kõik on kohal. Kes alustab? | Well, everybody's here. Who's starting? | |
| 2170 | Conversation, Tom | Mina alustan. Minu meelest on lõpp täiesti selge: Elsa jääb koju. | I'll start. To my mind the ending is perfectly clear: Elsa stays at home. | |
| 2171 | Conversation, Liis | Kuidas nii? Ta võttis ju kohvri ja läks bussi... | How come? She took her suitcase and got on the bus... | |
| 2172 | Conversation, Anna | ...oota, seda raamatus ei ole! Seal on ainult kirjas, et buss tuli ja Elsa võttis kohvri. | ...hang on, that isn't in the book! All it says is that the bus came and Elsa took her suitcase. | |
| 2173 | Conversation, Kati | Täpselt. Autor jätab lõpu lahtiseks ja lugeja peab ise otsustama. | Exactly. The author leaves the ending open, and the reader has to decide. | |
| 2174 | Conversation, Liis | Hea küll, selles on sul õigus. Aga miks ta siis kohvri kätte võttis? | All right, you've got a point there. But then why did she pick up the suitcase? | |
| 2175 | Conversation, Tom | Sest buss tuli. See ei tähenda veel, et ta... | Because the bus came. That doesn't mean she... | |
| 2176 | Conversation, Anna | ...et ta sõitis poja juurde Rootsi. Enne arvasin ma nagu Liis, aga nüüd ma ei ole enam kindel. | ...that she went to her son's in Sweden. I thought the same as Liis before, but now I'm not so sure. | |
| 2177 | Conversation, Kati | Hea raamat on just selline: iga lugeja loeb sealt välja oma lõpu. | That's exactly what a good book is like: every reader finds their own ending in it. | |
| 2178 | Conversation, Liis | Mina jään ikka oma arvamuse juurde. Elsa tahtis lõpuks poja lähedal olla. | I'm sticking to my view. In the end Elsa wanted to be near her son. | |
| 2179 | Conversation, Tom | Ja mina oma arvamuse juurde. Aga järgmisel kuul loeme midagi, mille lõpp on selge! | And I'm sticking to mine. But next month we're reading something with a clear ending! | |
| 2180 | Conversation, Kati | Näiteks kokaraamatut. | A cookbook, for example. | |

| 2198 | How it works | jätab, jääb, jätma, jääma | English has one 'leave' and Estonian has two: the author {jätab} the ending open, and the ending {jääb} open. Somebody doing it takes {jätma}; a thing staying that way takes {jääma}. | |
| 2199 | How it works | Jätma, autor jätab lõpu lahtiseks, Jääma, lõpp jääb lahtiseks, lahtiseks | {Jätma} has somebody doing it and a thing it's done to: {autor jätab lõpu lahtiseks}. {Jääma} has nobody: {lõpp jääb lahtiseks}. Both take the [saav] (-ks, becoming): {lahtiseks}. | |
| 2200 | How it works | autor jätab, autor vihjab, autor ei ütle | Talking about how a book is written, rather than what happens in it, the author is the one doing things: {autor jätab}, {autor vihjab}, {autor ei ütle}. | |
| 2201 | How it works | Ma jään oma arvamuse juurde, Ma ei ole enam kindel | Holding your view or changing it is said out loud: {Ma jään oma arvamuse juurde}, I'm sticking to my view, and {Ma ei ole enam kindel}, I'm not so sure anymore. | |
| 2202 | How it works | Elsa võttis kohvri, autor ei ütle, kas ta sõitis | Keep the character and the author apart: {Elsa võttis kohvri}, the character did it, but {autor ei ütle, kas ta sõitis}, the author is the one who doesn't say. | |
| 2203 | How it works, table | Autor jätab lõpu lahtiseks. | the author, The author leaves the ending open. | |
| 2204 | How it works, table | Lõpp jääb lahtiseks. | nobody: it just stays, The ending stays open. | |
| 2205 | How it works, table | Lugeja peab ise otsustama. | the reader, The reader has to decide. | |

| 2208 | Say it, question | Elsa sõitis ju ära! | But Elsa went away! | |
| 2209 | Say it, model answer | Selles on sul õigus, aga ma jään oma arvamuse juurde. | You've got a point there, but I'm sticking to my view. | |
| 2210 | Say it, model answer | Enne arvasin ma nii, aga nüüd ma ei ole enam kindel. | I thought so before, but now I'm not so sure. | |
| 2211 | Say it, question | Kes on raamatu peategelane? | Who is the book's main character? | |
| 2212 | Say it, model answer | Peategelane on Elsa, vana naine, kes elab maal oma majas. | The main character is Elsa, an old woman who lives in her own house in the country. | |

#### C1 44. Would you tell her? (`c1-44-ethics`)

| # | Where | Estonian | English | OK / fix |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 2213 | Conversation, Liis | Mul on teile üks küsimus. Mida te teeksite, kui te oleksite mina? | I've got a question for you. What would you do if you were me? | |
| 2214 | Conversation, Liis | Minu vana sõber Kerli kirjutas romaani ja tahab käsikirja kirjastusele saata. | My old friend Kerli has written a novel and wants to send the manuscript to a publisher. | |
| 2215 | Conversation, Liis | Ta palus, et ma loeksin selle läbi ja ütleksin ausalt, mida ma arvan. | She asked me to read it through and tell her honestly what I think. | |
| 2216 | Conversation, Tom | Ja? | And? | |
| 2217 | Conversation, Liis | Ja see on nõrk. Ausalt öeldes väga nõrk. | And it's weak. Honestly, very weak. | |
| 2218 | Conversation, Jaan | Siis ütle talle seda. Ta ju palus, et sa ütleksid ausalt. | Then tell her so. She did ask you to be honest. | |
| 2219 | Conversation, Anna | Oota, Jaan. Kui sa ütled sõbrale, et tema unistus on nõrk... | Hang on, Jaan. If you tell a friend their dream is weak... | |
| 2220 | Conversation, Tom | ...siis ta ei kirjuta enam kunagi midagi. Aga kui Liis valetab, saadab Kerli käsikirja ära ja saab kirjastusest kirja, et see neile ei sobi. | ...then they'll never write anything again. But if Liis lies, Kerli sends off the manuscript and gets a letter from the publisher saying it's not for them. | |
| 2221 | Conversation, Jaan | Täpselt. Valetamine ei aita kedagi. Ja mul oleks pärast halb südametunnistus. | Exactly. Lying doesn't help anybody. And I'd have a bad conscience afterwards. | |
| 2222 | Conversation, Anna | Ma ei räägi valetamisest. Võib öelda, mis on hea, ja jätta ülejäänu ütlemata. | I'm not talking about lying. You can say what's good and leave the rest unsaid. | |
| 2223 | Conversation, Liis | Hea küll, Anna, sa veensid mind poolenisti. | All right, Anna, you've half convinced me. | |
| 2224 | Conversation, Jaan | Ainult poolenisti? | Only half? | |
| 2225 | Conversation, Liis | Ma mõtlesin ümber. Ma ei valeta, aga ma ei ütle ka kõike korraga. Kõigepealt räägin sellest, mis oli hea. | I've changed my mind. I won't lie, but I won't say everything at once either. First I'll talk about what was good. | |
| 2226 | Conversation, Tom | Nii me teeme tööl ka: enne hea, siis see, mis võiks parem olla, ja lõpuks jälle hea. | That's what we do at work too: the good first, then what could be better, and the good again at the end. | |
| 2227 | Conversation, Jaan | Selles on sul õigus. Aga mina jään siiski oma arvamuse juurde: lõpuks peab ta teadma, mida parandada. | You've got a point there. But I'm still sticking to my view: in the end she has to know what to fix. | |
| 2228 | Conversation, Liis | Seda ta saab ka teada. Ta tunneb mind ju. | She'll find that out too. She knows me, after all. | |
