| 2248 | How it works | sa veensid mind, ma mõtlesin ümber, aga ... siiski | Changing your mind out loud is something a fluent speaker does well: say who moved you ({sa veensid mind}), say you've come round ({ma mõtlesin ümber}), and say what you still keep ({aga ... siiski}). | |
| 2249 | How it works | Ümber mõtlema, ümber, ma mõtlesin ümber | {Ümber mõtlema} is thinking it over and coming out the other side, and the {ümber} goes after the verb: {ma mõtlesin ümber}. | |
| 2250 | How it works | Veenma, sa veensid mind, te veensite mind, poolenisti | {Veenma} takes the person in the [osastav] (3rd form): {sa veensid mind}, {te veensite mind}. And {poolenisti} says how far: half. | |
| 2251 | How it works | Mida te teeksite?, Ma ütleksin talle, et, palus, et ma loeksin | What you'd do in somebody's place is the would form: {Mida te teeksite?}, {Ma ütleksin talle}. And what you're asked for is the would form after {et}: {palus, et ma loeksin}. | |
| 2252 | How it works | Ma mõtlesin ümber, vabandust | Coming round is no defeat in Estonian either: {Ma mõtlesin ümber} is said plainly, with no {vabandust} in front of it. Grant the point first and keep what you keep, and the argument stays friendly. | |
| 2253 | How it works, table | Selles on sul õigus. | grant a point, You've got a point there. | |
| 2254 | How it works, table | Sa veensid mind poolenisti. | say who moved you, You've half convinced me. | |
| 2255 | How it works, table | Ma mõtlesin ümber. | come round, I've changed my mind. | |
| 2256 | How it works, table | Aga mina jään siiski oma arvamuse juurde. | keep your position, But I'm still sticking to my view. | |
| 2257 | Say it, question | Mida sina teeksid? | What would you do? | |
| 2258 | Say it, model answer | Ma ütleksin talle ausalt, aga kõigepealt räägiksin sellest, mis oli hea. | I'd tell her honestly, but first I'd talk about what was good. | |
| 2259 | Say it, question | Kas sa oled nüüd nõus? | Do you agree now? | |
| 2260 | Say it, model answer | Sa veensid mind. Ma mõtlesin ümber. | You've convinced me. I've changed my mind. | |
| 2261 | Say it, question | Kas valetamine on vahel õige? | Is lying sometimes right? | |
| 2262 | Say it, model answer | Vahel see küll aitab, aga lõpuks ei aita valetamine kedagi. | Sometimes it helps, granted, but in the end lying helps nobody. | |
| 2263 | Say it, model answer | Sa veensid mind poolenisti. | You've half convinced me. | |

#### C1 45. Almost the same word (`c1-45-almost`)

| # | Where | Estonian | English | OK / fix |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 2264 | Conversation, Õppejõud | Tere õhtust! Täna räägime sellest, mis sõnad käivad koos. | Good evening! Today we're talking about words that go together. | |
| 2265 | Conversation, Õppejõud | Võtame näiteks otsuse. Otsust saab teha, aga kirjas otsust ka langetatakse. | Take a decision, for example. You can make a decision, but in writing a decision is also 'lowered'. | |
| 2266 | Conversation, Irina | Langetatakse? Ma arvasin, et langetatakse ainult pead... | Lowered? I thought you could only lower your head... | |
| 2267 | Conversation, Anna | ...või hinda! Poes langetatakse vahel hinda. | ...or a price! Shops lower their prices sometimes. | |
| 2268 | Conversation, Õppejõud | Väga hea! Sõna on sama, aga otsusega koos tähendab see, et otsus on suur ja raske. Kodus öeldakse lihtsalt: me otsustasime. | Very good! The word is the same, but with a decision it means the decision is a big, hard one. At home you just say: we decided. | |
| 2269 | Conversation, Irina | Ja kuidas on küsimusega? Küsimust küsitakse? | And what about a question? Is a question asked? | |
| 2270 | Conversation, Õppejõud | Kodus küll. Kirjas aga küsimust esitatakse. Ja kui küsimus on uus ja raske, siis see tõstatatakse. | At home, yes. But in writing a question is put. And if a question is new and difficult, it's raised. | |
| 2271 | Conversation, Anna | Tõstatatakse... Mul on tööl üks kolleeg, kes tõstatab igal koosolekul uue küsimuse... | Raised... I've got a colleague at work who raises a new question at every meeting... | |
| 2272 | Conversation, Irina | ...ja koosolek kestab veel kaks tundi! | ...and the meeting goes on for another two hours! | |
| 2273 | Conversation, Õppejõud | Just nii. Ja nüüd kaks sõna, mida sõnaraamat tõlgib ühtemoodi: põhjus ja põhjendus. | Exactly. And now two words the dictionary translates the same way: a reason and a justification. | |
| 2274 | Conversation, Anna | Minu sõnaraamatus on neil sama tõlge. | My dictionary gives them the same translation. | |
| 2275 | Conversation, Õppejõud | Aga vahe on suur. Põhjus on see, miks midagi juhtus. Põhjendus on see, mida inimene ütleb, kui ta oma otsust selgitab. | But there's a big difference. A reason is why something happened. A justification is what somebody says when they explain their decision. | |
| 2276 | Conversation, Irina | Nii et kui ma tulen tundi hilja, on põhjus see, et ma magasin sisse... | So if I come to class late, the reason is that I overslept... | |
| 2277 | Conversation, Anna | ...aga põhjendus on see, et buss ei tulnud! | ...but the justification is that the bus didn't come! | |
| 2278 | Conversation, Õppejõud | Täpselt. Järgmiseks tunniks kirjutage, mis vahe neil on. Keeles mängib iga sõna väga suurt rolli. | Exactly. For next time, write down what the difference between them is. In a language, every word plays a very big part. | |

A decision is made, {tehakse}, at home and lowered, {langetatakse}, in writing; a question is asked at home and put, {esitatakse}, in writing, and a new one raised, {tõstatatakse}; and a thing plays a part, {mängib rolli}. | |

#### C1 46. A review of a book (`c1-46-review`)

| # | Where | Estonian | English | OK / fix |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 2316 | Conversation, Kati | Tom, kuule, mul on sulle üks palve. Raamatukogu tahab meie klubist arvustust Elsa raamatu kohta. | Tom, listen, I've got a favor to ask. The library wants a review of Elsa's book from our club. | |
| 2317 | Conversation, Tom | Arvustust? Ma ei ole kunagi eesti keeles arvustust kirjutanud. | A review? I've never written a review in Estonian. | |
| 2318 | Conversation, Kati | Pole hullu. Kirjuta nii, nagu sa klubis rääkisid. Sina põhjendasid oma arvamust kõige paremini. | No problem. Write it the way you talked at the club. You gave the best reasons for your view. | |
| 2319 | Conversation, Anna | Ja ära reeda lõppu! | And don't give the ending away! | |
| 2320 | Conversation, Tom | Aga mis arvustuses üldse olema peab? | But what actually has to be in a review? | |
| 2321 | Conversation, Kati | Esiteks, millest raamat räägib, aga lühidalt. Teiseks, kuidas see on kirjutatud... | First, what the book is about, but briefly. Second, how it's written... | |
| 2322 | Conversation, Anna | ...ja kolmandaks, kas sa soovitad seda ja kellele. | ...and third, whether you'd recommend it, and to whom. | |
| 2323 | Conversation, Tom | Ja kas ma võin kirjutada, et alguses oli igav? | And can I write that the beginning was boring? | |
| 2324 | Conversation, Kati | Võid küll, kui sa põhjendad, miks. | Of course you can, if you give the reason why. | |
| 2325 | Conversation, Tom | Põhjendus on mul olemas: alguses ei toimu mitte midagi. | I've got a reason: nothing whatsoever happens at the start. | |
| 2326 | Conversation, Anna | Aga just see oli ju ilus! Elsa on oma majas ja seal on ainult vaikus... | But that's exactly what was beautiful! Elsa's in her house, and there's only silence there... | |
| 2327 | Conversation, Tom | ...ja vaikus, jah. Hea küll, sa veensid mind. Ma kirjutan, et algus on aeglane, aga mõjuv. | ...and silence, yes. All right, you've convinced me. I'll write that the beginning is slow, but powerful. | |
| 2328 | Conversation, Kati | Just nii! Ja kirjuta umbes poolteist lehekülge, mitte rohkem. | That's it! And write about a page and a half, no more. | |
| 2329 | Conversation, Tom | Poolteist lehekülge eesti keeles? See on minu jaoks juba romaan! | A page and a half in Estonian? For me that's a novel already! | |

| 2332 | Words (form) | arvustust | a review, as an object: {arvustust kirjutama} | |

| 2334 | Words (verb, I form) | põhjendan | I give my reasons | |
| 2335 | Words (form) | põhjendasin | I gave reasons; {sa põhjendasid}, you gave reasons | |

| 2350 | How it works | sest, minu meelest ... , kuna | A review has a shape: what the book is about, briefly; how it's written; a reason for every opinion, {sest} or {minu meelest ... , kuna}; and who it's for. And it never gives the ending away. | |
| 2351 | How it works | tegevus, meeleolu, tugev külg | A review is about how a book is written as much as what happens: {tegevus} is what happens, {meeleolu} is what it feels like, and {tugev külg} is what works. | |
| 2352 | How it works | põhjendama, Algus on igav, Algus on aeglane, sest... | Every opinion comes with its reason, which is {põhjendama}: {Algus on igav} is a complaint, {Algus on aeglane, sest...} is a review. | |
| 2353 | How it works | Reetma, Rohkem ma ei reeda | {Reetma} is to betray, and a review that tells the ending betrays the reader: {Rohkem ma ei reeda}. | |
| 2354 | How it works | Mulle meeldis | {Mulle meeldis} on its own is not a review: say what you liked and why. An Estonian reader trusts a review that names a weak side as well as a strong one. | |

| 2358 | How it works, table | Rohkem ma ei reeda. | the ending, not given away, I won't give away any more. | |
| 2359 | How it works, table | Soovitan seda kõigile, kes tahavad pärast lugemist veel mõelda. | who it's for, I recommend it to anybody who wants to go on thinking after they've read it. | |
| 2360 | Say it, question | Millest see raamat räägib? | What's this book about? | |
| 2361 | Say it, model answer | Peategelane on Elsa, vana naine, kes peab otsustama, kas ta jääb koju või sõidab poja juurde. Rohkem ma ei reeda. | The main character is Elsa, an old woman who has to decide whether to stay at home or go to her son's. I won't give away any more. | |
| 2362 | Say it, question | Kas sulle raamat meeldis? | Did you like the book? | |
| 2363 | Say it, model answer | Algus on aeglane, aga mõjuv, sest vaikus on seal sama tähtis kui sõnad. | The beginning is slow but powerful, because silence matters as much as words in it. | |
| 2364 | Say it, question | Kellele sa seda soovitaksid? | Who would you recommend it to? | |
| 2365 | Say it, model answer | Soovitan seda kõigile, kes tahavad pärast lugemist veel mõelda. | I recommend it to anybody who wants to go on thinking after reading it. | |

#### C1 47. Ten ideas in ten minutes (`c1-47-lecture`)

| # | Where | Estonian | English | OK / fix |

| 2366 | Conversation, Margit | Meiega on täna Riina Mets. Riina on keeleteadlane ja uurib, kuidas täiskasvanud uut keelt õpivad. | With us today is Riina Mets. Riina is a linguist, and she studies how adults learn a new language. | |
| 2367 | Conversation, Tõnu | Tere tulemast, Riina! Kas täiskasvanu saab üldse veel uue keele selgeks? | Welcome, Riina! Can an adult still really learn a new language at all? | |
| 2368 | Conversation, Riina | Saab küll. Ja tihti kiiremini, kui arvatakse. Aga ta õpib teisiti kui laps. | Yes, they can. And often faster than people think. But an adult learns differently from a child. | |
| 2369 | Conversation, Anna | Tom, tule kuula! Siin räägitakse sinust. | Tom, come and listen! They're talking about you. | |
| 2370 | Conversation, Tom | Minust? Mida nad minust räägivad? | About me? What are they saying about me? | |
| 2371 | Conversation, Anna | Et täiskasvanu õpib... | That an adult learns... | |
| 2372 | Conversation, Margit | ...teisiti kui laps. Riina, mida see tähendab? | ...differently from a child. Riina, what does that mean? | |
| 2373 | Conversation, Riina | Just sellest ma täna räägin. Mul on teile kümme mõtet. | That's just what I'm talking about today. I've got ten ideas for you. | |
| 2374 | Conversation, Tom | Kümme! Kus on paber? Ma teen märkmeid. | Ten! Where's some paper? I'm taking notes. | |
| 2375 | Conversation, Anna | Sina? Märkmeid? Raadio jaoks? | You? Notes? For the radio? | |
| 2376 | Conversation, Tom | Eksamil tuleb ka loeng, mida kuulatakse ainult üks kord. Parem harjutada nüüd. | There's a lecture in the exam too, and you only hear it once. Better to practice now. | |
| 2377 | Conversation, Anna | Hea küll. Siis teen mina ka. | All right. Then I'll take some too. | |

Töötan programmeerijana ja kevadest saadik olen meie meeskonna juht. | I'm Tom, I'm from England, and I've lived in Estonia for several years now. I work as a programmer, and since the spring I've led our team. | |

## 2. Recordings to make

### The known retakes

Each of these is a native recording whose text a native speaker would not say in that place, so the text was
left alone. Record the line again with the text the native speaker settles on, change the lesson to match, and
give the new take a new id in `lib/audio/native.ts`.

- `a1-12-saying-no`: Tom answers `Ei, ma ei ela Tallinnas. Ma elan Tartus.` Everywhere else in the course Tom
  lives in Tallinn. For instance, Jaan asks about Tartu and Tom answers `Ei, ma ei ela Tartus. Ma elan Tallinnas.`
- `a1-10a-neighbours`: Jaan's `Jah. Aga minu õpetaja on pärit Lätist. Ta on lätlane.` The `Aga` contrasts
  nothing. For instance `Jah, olen. Minu õpetaja on pärit Lätist. Ta on lätlane.`
- `a1-11-languages`: Tom's `Ma räägin inglise keelt. Ja natuke eesti keelt!` repeats what he has just said a line
  earlier. For instance `Mina räägin inglise keelt.`
- `a1-12-saying-no`, the model answer `Ma ei räägi veel hästi eesti keelt.`: a native puts it `Ma ei räägi eesti
  keelt veel hästi.`
- `a1-10-from`: Tom's `Anna on pärit Ukrainast. Ta on ukrainlane.` says one fact twice; `Anna on pärit Ukrainast.`
  is enough. Optional.
- `a1-04-persons`: Tom says `Ma olen õpilane.`, and later lessons make him an adult with two children. Whether
  `õpilane` is right for an adult on a language course, or `õppija` or a job fits better, is the native
  speaker's call. Lesson 15's `Aga mul on laps!` (not recorded) also gives Tom one child where lesson 16 gives
  him two.
- The `a1-trepil` scene's pick `Ei, ma ei ole eestlane. Ma olen inglane.` reads as a textbook answer; in speech
  `Ei, ma pole eestlane, olen inglane.`
- `a1-09`: the sweep removed Tom's line `Mina elan ka siin!`, so its recording is no longer played anywhere.

### Not recorded yet

Conversation lines are listed only for the men's parts (Tom, Jaan, Peeter), since the recordings are one man's
voice and he never plays a woman's part. Add a recorded line to `NATIVE_CLIPS`, raise `NATIVE_CUT`, and add
Peeter to `NATIVE_PARTS` once his lines are in.
